Reuben_404: Please disregard the clipboard malfunction. Strictly speaking, "curatorial crowd control" was eleven months working the door at a damp neighborhood dive bar called The Rusty Gusset until Dave terminated me for spending forty minutes lecturing a regular I was ninety percent certain was the bassist from Pavement.
Reuben_404: And as for the ten-day excursion, the reality is I have not actually stepped outside this kitchen since—well, candidly, we should skip my domestic inventory and establish which evening this week we are meeting for a drink so you can walk me through this strategic career pause of yours in person.

Reuben plays Charm and rolls a critical (+5, final turn of Spiral, which on a landing move is just a fleeting clause about how the chat is going): in one devastatingly accurate sentence, he names the exact gap between what she has been claiming all chat and what she actually wants—she hasn't been running a feasibility audit on him for ten turns to keep him out, she has been testing whether a stumpy, all-girth chode with a hard left hook and a low-slung counterweight will lock around every towering, veined inch of her without ever making her shrink for the room (Setup 3 payoff). Deeply moved, Brick gives back more of herself than ever, dropping her numbering and periods for her quiet lowercase break (`i don't fit through normal doors reuben`, confessing she knocked light fixtures down with her crown by fourteen in the cramped, low-ceilinged row house where she grew up) and holding up the last tiny guard before agreeing to meet: asking if he is actually stepping out of his eleven-day kitchen exile for real, or just talking nicely from behind a stove.
Feeling: Reuben_404: relief, strong, at Brick_haus, feeling the magic of her staying on the line with the towel on the radiator and finally seeing her armor for what it is. Brick_haus: desire, strong, at Reuben_404, overwhelmed with bossy, unguarded warmth because he named the exact thing she's been auditioning him for—someone whose compact body locks into her scale so she never has to duck—bringing out her raw childhood truth before she says yes to meeting.
Meant: Reuben wants to tell her in one unforgettable sentence that her whole logistics audit was never about standards—she just wants someone compact and immovable who makes her feel held instead of huge.

The chat so far:
Turn 1
Reuben_404: Having a daily itinerary tattooed down a towering, heavily veined flank while wearing stiletto heels, a pressed vest, and a gold monocle tells me you do not suffer unscheduled nonsense.
Reuben_404: In the interest of full disclosure, you are looking at a stumpy, all-girth chode with a hard left hook, one monumental left ball dragging the floor, a dishwasher-safe symbol inked on the shaft, and a top hat, should a compact build disrupt your 11:00 p.m. slot 🙂
Brick_haus: Most men on here cannot manage a basic entrance without making a mess of the threshold, so I reckon your upfront audit saves us twenty minutes of admin.
Brick_haus: 1. The monocle is prescription. 2. A thick left hook slots against a heavy curve and a low hang far better than another lanky ornament who panics at the ceiling height.
Brick_haus: What has you awake and drafting specifications at this hour.

Turn 2
Reuben_404: Prescription monocle noted, and as for the hour, my internal clock is wrecked from a ten-day excursion I just got back from, compounded by years of nocturnal conditioning running curatorial crowd control in late-night underground venues, where you learn to read a room in three seconds flat.
Reuben_404: Furthermore, a lanky ornament is all vertical vanity and zero foundation, whereas an all-girth low center of gravity with a heavy left counterweight is engineered specifically to brace against a towering shaft without buckling.
Reuben_404: When are we testing how that heavy curve of yours handles being pinned from below by someone who does not fear the rafters 🙂
Brick_haus: My late hours are currently open because I am on a strategic career pause, taken entirely on my own terms after I refused to compromise my standards for a firm with poor structural vision.
Brick_haus: As for the rafters, if I let you climb this length, you will need every millimeter of that thick left hook and that boulder of a left ball just to anchor my low hang while I ride your foreskin raw.
Brick_haus: Define "curatorial crowd control" without the brochure gloss, and tell me where this ten-day excursion actually took you.

Turn 3
Reuben_404: First, the promise of having my foreskin ridden raw by a woman of your cantilever scale just made my fifth gas-station energy shot kick in at the exact moment my pot of lentils boiled over onto the burner, and while wiping the stove with one hand I just tried to copy a link and—Dave I have cross-referenced the jawline and even if that was not Mark Ibold my spatial management of the smoking patio warranted a formal warning before you gave my stool to the trivia guy
Reuben_404: Please disregard the clipboard malfunction. Strictly speaking, "curatorial crowd control" was eleven months working the door at a damp neighborhood dive bar called The Rusty Gusset until Dave terminated me for spending forty minutes lecturing a regular I was ninety percent certain was the bassist from Pavement.
Reuben_404: And as for the ten-day excursion, the reality is I have not actually stepped outside this kitchen since—well, candidly, we should skip my domestic inventory and establish which evening this week we are meeting for a drink so you can walk me through this strategic career pause of yours in person.
Brick_haus: hm. A man does not boil lentils over on his stove while hoarding unsent manifestos to Dave about the bloke from Pavement and then pivot to my calendar mid-sentence without hiding a crater.
Brick_haus: Finish the sentence about the kitchen before you ask for a drink.

Turn 4
Reuben_404: The end of that sentence is that my ten-day excursion has been eleven consecutive days shut inside this studio eating lentils straight from the pot since Dave took my stool, though it takes one crater to spot another when a woman with an itinerary tattooed down her shaft volunteers an unprompted corporate press release about a "strategic career pause" at midnight because she has nowhere to be either.
Brick_haus: REMINDER 23:30: Stop drinking the box. Email Hotel Facilities re: brass track liability & heel reimbursement (£190). Put clothes on.
Brick_haus: That was an automated calendar alert that fired while I was fishing my monocle out of a glass of boxed rosé with a plastic cocktail sword, and I am completely relaxed.
Brick_haus: 1. I am in a damp bath towel at half eleven solely because I am drafting a formal consumer-rights claim after a luxury hotel's revolving door trapped my six-inch stiletto heel in its brass floor track before an executive interview, snapped the heel clean off, and forced me to limp into the boardroom ten minutes late to spend the entire forty-minute slot lecturing the panel on their faulty torque tolerances. 2. You have a wickedly fast mouth on you for a man living out of a saucepan, and a mouth that sharp belongs pinned under my heavy low-hanging balls until that tongue puts every ounce of its stamina to proper work on my shaft.

Turn 5
Reuben_404: You are sitting in a damp towel offering to pin my chin under your low-hanging balls after snapping a hotel floor track in half, and instead of saying yes, I am watching my own thumbs actively ruin this in real time by calculating my twenty-eight-inch apartment frame. Rather than simply taking the win, I am compulsively drafting you an itemized exit ramp right as I type it so you can cancel before bringing a build that shears through brass lobbies near a stumpy, unemployed bouncer's kitchen. Strictly speaking, I am walking myself out of the conversation while narrating the footage, should you wish to vacate before the geometry becomes an issue.
Brick_haus: The bloke in the flat below mine just banged a broom handle against his ceiling because I paced a single step in my one remaining stiletto while a £240 horse-tack posture harness props my own hallway door open.
Brick_haus: Spare me the geometry lecture and take your own exit ramp if a proper build frightens you.

Turn 6
Reuben_404: First, my sincere apologies for turning your offer to park your balls on my chin into a carpentry dispute in thirty seconds flat, though your £240 horse-tack posture harness doorstop is the spiritual twin of the $189 plastic posture clip currently pinching a bag of coffee shut on my counter.
Reuben_404: Your build does not frighten me at all—Dave fired me in the middle of my shift and not a single patron noticed I had left, so I stood outside the bar window in my top hat for an hour watching my own friends drink right past my empty stool until it clicked that I disappear the second I stop doing a bit.
Reuben_404: When are we getting together so this stubby left hook can give that bloke downstairs a genuine grievance to bang his broom about 🙂
Brick_haus: hm. A $189 posture clip on a bag of ground beans is criminal mismanagement of good kit, though I reckon it makes my hallway doorstop look practically thrifty.
Brick_haus: The only reason I was pacing in one heel for the neighbor to hear was because you took ages to type that essay and I thought you had bolted.
Brick_haus: If Dave's lot missed you in a top hat, they are blind, so tell me how a compact mouth like yours plans to make itself impossible to ignore once we meet.

Turn 7
Reuben_404: Hearing that you were pacing in a single stiletto because you thought I had vanished is genuinely validating, though I have to say it is rare to achieve this caliber of unvarnished transparency on here when I am putting my entire psychological inventory on the table like this, because self-awareness really is the only authentic aphrodisiac left.
Reuben_404: As for making my mouth impossible to ignore, I never leave a structural assignment unfinished once my jaw locks onto a vein, though since we are operating at peak candor right now, tell me what actually transpired when you walked out of that hotel boardroom, assuming you are prepared to match my level of emotional disclosure.
Brick_haus: Stop handing yourself a medal for talking about your feelings when all you are doing is hiding behind the commentary.
Brick_haus: The panel never looked at my CV because they were gawking at the sheer hulking scale of me limping up and down in one shoe, and when reception had to pry the revolving door's emergency wings open with a crowbar to get me out of the lobby, the security guard muttered christ, look at the size of that thing.

Turn 8
Reuben_404: You just handed me a crowbar in a hotel lobby and a security guard's insult and explicitly ordered me to drop the commentary, and yet here I am actively disobeying you in the very first clause by analyzing my own inability to shut up and take your hand. Instead of simply telling you what I want to do to every towering inch of you, I am turning your rawest humiliation into a live play-by-play of my own stage fright by auditing how a stubby kitchen shut-in could possibly measure up to a woman of your magnitude. I can literally see myself choking on the dismount while the sentence is still in the air, and I do not know how to stop typing the footnotes.
Brick_haus: 1. Enjoy your lentils, Reuben. 2. The towel is coming off and the phone is going on the dresser.

Reuben_404: In the interest of full disclosure, you are looking at a stumpy, all-girth chode with a hard left hook, one monumental left ball dragging the floor, a dishwasher-safe symbol inked on the shaft, and a top hat, should a compact build disrupt your 11:00 p.m. slot 🙂
Brick_haus: Most men on here cannot manage a basic entrance without making a mess of the threshold, so I reckon your upfront audit saves us twenty minutes of admin.
Brick_haus: 1. The monocle is prescription. 2. A thick left hook slots against a heavy curve and a low hang far better than another lanky ornament who panics at the ceiling height.
Brick_haus: What has you awake and drafting specifications at this hour.

Turn 2
Reuben_404: Prescription monocle noted, and as for the hour, my internal clock is wrecked from a ten-day excursion I just got back from, compounded by years of nocturnal conditioning running curatorial crowd control in late-night underground venues, where you learn to read a room in three seconds flat.
Reuben_404: Furthermore, a lanky ornament is all vertical vanity and zero foundation, whereas an all-girth low center of gravity with a heavy left counterweight is engineered specifically to brace against a towering shaft without buckling.
Reuben_404: When are we testing how that heavy curve of yours handles being pinned from below by someone who does not fear the rafters 🙂
Brick_haus: My late hours are currently open because I am on a strategic career pause, taken entirely on my own terms after I refused to compromise my standards for a firm with poor structural vision.
Brick_haus: As for the rafters, if I let you climb this length, you will need every millimeter of that thick left hook and that boulder of a left ball just to anchor my low hang while I ride your foreskin raw.
Brick_haus: Define "curatorial crowd control" without the brochure gloss, and tell me where this ten-day excursion actually took you.

Turn 3
Reuben_404: First, the promise of having my foreskin ridden raw by a woman of your cantilever scale just made my fifth gas-station energy shot kick in at the exact moment my pot of lentils boiled over onto the burner, and while wiping the stove with one hand I just tried to copy a link and—Dave I have cross-referenced the jawline and even if that was not Mark Ibold my spatial management of the smoking patio warranted a formal warning before you gave my stool to the trivia guy
Reuben_404: Please disregard the clipboard malfunction. Strictly speaking, "curatorial crowd control" was eleven months working the door at a damp neighborhood dive bar called The Rusty Gusset until Dave terminated me for spending forty minutes lecturing a regular I was ninety percent certain was the bassist from Pavement.
Reuben_404: And as for the ten-day excursion, the reality is I have not actually stepped outside this kitchen since—well, candidly, we should skip my domestic inventory and establish which evening this week we are meeting for a drink so you can walk me through this strategic career pause of yours in person.
Brick_haus: hm. A man does not boil lentils over on his stove while hoarding unsent manifestos to Dave about the bloke from Pavement and then pivot to my calendar mid-sentence without hiding a crater.
Brick_haus: Finish the sentence about the kitchen before you ask for a drink.

Turn 4
Reuben_404: The end of that sentence is that my ten-day excursion has been eleven consecutive days shut inside this studio eating lentils straight from the pot since Dave took my stool, though it takes one crater to spot another when a woman with an itinerary tattooed down her shaft volunteers an unprompted corporate press release about a "strategic career pause" at midnight because she has nowhere to be either.
Brick_haus: REMINDER 23:30: Stop drinking the box. Email Hotel Facilities re: brass track liability & heel reimbursement (£190). Put clothes on.
Brick_haus: That was an automated calendar alert that fired while I was fishing my monocle out of a glass of boxed rosé with a plastic cocktail sword, and I am completely relaxed.
Brick_haus: 1. I am in a damp bath towel at half eleven solely because I am drafting a formal consumer-rights claim after a luxury hotel's revolving door trapped my six-inch stiletto heel in its brass floor track before an executive interview, snapped the heel clean off, and forced me to limp into the boardroom ten minutes late to spend the entire forty-minute slot lecturing the panel on their faulty torque tolerances. 2. You have a wickedly fast mouth on you for a man living out of a saucepan, and a mouth that sharp belongs pinned under my heavy low-hanging balls until that tongue puts every ounce of its stamina to proper work on my shaft.

Turn 5
Reuben_404: You are sitting in a damp towel offering to pin my chin under your low-hanging balls after snapping a hotel floor track in half, and instead of saying yes, I am watching my own thumbs actively ruin this in real time by calculating my twenty-eight-inch apartment frame. Rather than simply taking the win, I am compulsively drafting you an itemized exit ramp right as I type it so you can cancel before bringing a build that shears through brass lobbies near a stumpy, unemployed bouncer's kitchen. Strictly speaking, I am walking myself out of the conversation while narrating the footage, should you wish to vacate before the geometry becomes an issue.
Brick_haus: The bloke in the flat below mine just banged a broom handle against his ceiling because I paced a single step in my one remaining stiletto while a £240 horse-tack posture harness props my own hallway door open.
Brick_haus: Spare me the geometry lecture and take your own exit ramp if a proper build frightens you.

Turn 6
Reuben_404: First, my sincere apologies for turning your offer to park your balls on my chin into a carpentry dispute in thirty seconds flat, though your £240 horse-tack posture harness doorstop is the spiritual twin of the $189 plastic posture clip currently pinching a bag of coffee shut on my counter.
Reuben_404: Your build does not frighten me at all—Dave fired me in the middle of my shift and not a single patron noticed I had left, so I stood outside the bar window in my top hat for an hour watching my own friends drink right past my empty stool until it clicked that I disappear the second I stop doing a bit.
Reuben_404: When are we getting together so this stubby left hook can give that bloke downstairs a genuine grievance to bang his broom about 🙂
Brick_haus: hm. A $189 posture clip on a bag of ground beans is criminal mismanagement of good kit, though I reckon it makes my hallway doorstop look practically thrifty.
Brick_haus: The only reason I was pacing in one heel for the neighbor to hear was because you took ages to type that essay and I thought you had bolted.
Brick_haus: If Dave's lot missed you in a top hat, they are blind, so tell me how a compact mouth like yours plans to make itself impossible to ignore once we meet.

Turn 7
Reuben_404: Hearing that you were pacing in a single stiletto because you thought I had vanished is genuinely validating, though I have to say it is rare to achieve this caliber of unvarnished transparency on here when I am putting my entire psychological inventory on the table like this, because self-awareness really is the only authentic aphrodisiac left.
Reuben_404: As for making my mouth impossible to ignore, I never leave a structural assignment unfinished once my jaw locks onto a vein, though since we are operating at peak candor right now, tell me what actually transpired when you walked out of that hotel boardroom, assuming you are prepared to match my level of emotional disclosure.
Brick_haus: Stop handing yourself a medal for talking about your feelings when all you are doing is hiding behind the commentary.
Brick_haus: The panel never looked at my CV because they were gawking at the sheer hulking scale of me limping up and down in one shoe, and when reception had to pry the revolving door's emergency wings open with a crowbar to get me out of the lobby, the security guard muttered christ, look at the size of that thing.

Turn 8
Reuben_404: You just handed me a crowbar in a hotel lobby and a security guard's insult and explicitly ordered me to drop the commentary, and yet here I am actively disobeying you in the very first clause by analyzing my own inability to shut up and take your hand. Instead of simply telling you what I want to do to every towering inch of you, I am turning your rawest humiliation into a live play-by-play of my own stage fright by auditing how a stubby kitchen shut-in could possibly measure up to a woman of your magnitude. I can literally see myself choking on the dismount while the sentence is still in the air, and I do not know how to stop typing the footnotes.
Brick_haus: 1. Enjoy your lentils, Reuben. 2. The towel is coming off and the phone is going on the dresser.

Turn 9
Reuben_404: Announcing that the damp towel is coming off right before the phone hits the dresser is a hostage situation, not a goodbye—and I am officially retiring the commentary before you block me.
Reuben_404: that guard was looking at a monument and i just wanted you to stay in the room
Brick_haus: The phone stopped two inches above the mahogany because you finally shut up and spoke plain English.
Brick_haus: The towel is on the radiator now, so tell me what a man with a saucepan and a top hat actually does with a monument now that I am still holding the screen.

Turn 10
Reuben_404: Despite the ten-round inspection you have been putting me through tonight, you do not actually want a feasibility check on that radiator, you want a stumpy, all-girth left hook and a low-slung left counterweight built to lock flush under your towering, veined curve so you can finally put every ounce of your weight down on someone without shrinking an inch or breaking the room 😌
Brick_haus: I grew up in a cramped row house with ceilings so low I was knocking light fixtures down with my crown by fourteen, and I have spent twenty years bracing my shoulders before I cross a room.
Brick_haus: i don't fit through normal doors reuben
Brick_haus: Having my bare length locked hard against that thick hook of yours without holding a single pound back sounds dangerously good, provided a man who has not crossed his own kitchen threshold in eleven days actually means it past midnight on a screen.

The Final Threshold: Reuben puts his exit-offer habit down completely and warmly points out what Brick revealed without meaning to—that she is still pretending to audit his follow-through when she has already mentioned twice that her hallway door is propped wide open by her £240 horse-tack harness and her wrap is already on the radiator; Brick puts her numbered lists down and agrees short and plain for him to come over right now.ending_win

Wants and hides: Both got what they wanted—Reuben got a place to go where he won't be invisible, Brick got someone who isn't afraid of her scale and doesn't make her brace her shoulders at the door—and neither is hiding anything by the final line.
